# RUN LOG — Autonomous Top-Tier Run

> **Normative protocol for the long autonomous run.**
> `docs/AUTONOMOUS_TOP_TIER_RUN.md` defines **WHAT** (waves W0–W7, D1–D10, Briefs A–E).
> This file defines **HOW** sessions run, **what counts as evidence**, and **where the run stands**.
> The repo is the only cross-session memory. Chat dies with the context window — if it is not
> written here (or another committed file), it never happened.

---

## 0. Protocol — every session, in order

1. **Bootstrap.** Read `AGENTS.md` → `docs/AUTONOMOUS_TOP_TIER_RUN.md` → this file
   (Status header + tail of Session ledger). Run `./tools/ci.sh` once on the current tree.
2. **Audit the prior session.** Pick ONE claim from the last session report; re-run its
   cited command; compare raw output. Log PASS/FAIL. A failed audit marks downstream
   work *suspect* until re-verified (note it in Blocked/Dead-ends).
3. **Claim before work.** Set `Active claim:` in the Status header (session #, wave, ONE
   deliverable). If a stale claim exists (>1 session old), verify it or release it.
4. **Pre-register wave criteria.** When starting a new wave, fill its row in the Wave
   register *before* implementing — done means what the row said, not what is convenient.
5. **Implement the smallest complete slice** behind its evidence gate
   (player-path test / screenshot diff / perf number / save-hash).
6. **CI.** Run `./tools/ci.sh`; record the raw result.
7. **Report.** Append the session report (template in §10); update the Status header
   (done / next / blocked / gates / last-verified artifact); update `AGENTS.md` if a
   phase/SSOT fact changed; compact context at wave boundaries.
8. **Continue** unless a STOP condition (§3) fired.

---

## 1. Evidence contract

- Every "done" = **file path + exact command + raw output** (commit SHA, CI result, test
  counts, screenshot paths, perf numbers). Nothing else counts.
- **Never cite an earlier session's green** — re-run it yourself.
- **Screenshot claims**: path + which forced state + distinctness method (md5/diff).
- **Auto-fail fake-done blacklist**: "should work", "likely fine", edited thresholds,
  skipped/ignored/deleted tests, error suppression, claiming a gate without raw output.

## 2. Scoreboard is add-only

Tests, CI config, release thresholds and D1–D10 criteria may only be **strengthened**,
never weakened. If a test looks wrong: write a `Dispute` entry in §8, mark the item
blocked, and stop — editing the scoreboard is a human gate.

## 3. STOP conditions (halt the loop, write HANDOFF NEEDED)

- **Human gate reached** (plan §12): Steam account/depot credentials; playtest protocol;
  paid-service sign-off; capsule/trailer/price approval; legal; **any push to a remote**.
- **Any spend proposal** — always a human gate (free-first, plan §7).
- **Circuit breakers** (log, no human needed to trigger):
  - CI red in two consecutive sessions;
  - the same deliverable failed twice;
  - a target metric unmoved across two waves.
- **All remaining unblocked work exhausted.**

Geo stages 2-3 (docs/geo/STAGE23_PLAN.md) on geo/int: captain P0 landed; tracks A-D (geo/sim-data, geo/sea-candidates, geo/map-national, geo/map-region) cut; A1's foundation merge (the embedded geo readers, no engine wiring) landed with the captain's review fixes. Wave 4's graphics stages (gfx-farm-1, gfx-polish-1, gfx-farm-2) wait for the owner's OK to open windows — see docs/HANDOFF.md §6`
- **Active claim:** `geo stages 2-3, merge captain on geo/int (local only, never pushed until integration close); headless only; align with GitHub before resuming`
- **Gate state:** `geo/int after the A1 merge and review fixes (4df0391), headless on the shared Mac: dotnet 2177 passed / 3 skipped, pytest 761 passed / 1 skipped, scenes 0 failures, UI flow 1637 checks 0 failures, golden 27 of 27 identical against P0; the full tools/ci.sh (shader check, perf) is not run locally, GitHub CI runs it on the PR`
- **Last verified artifact:** `wave4/int: golden traces re-recorded once on the combined tree, 27 of 27 games identical to origin/main on every day, save drift only, every node explained (docs/run/evidence/wave4_int_golden_diff.md)`

### Session-0 note
After CI is green, create a **local baseline commit** (do NOT push — pushing is a human
gate) so every evidence claim can cite a SHA. From then on, one commit per completed
deliverable, message format: `run: <wave> <item> — <one-line outcome>`.

| 2026-10-14 | Merge-captain fix (wave1 integration, the heave part of G2-03): the G1-05 UI-flow check "a 25 m/s storm's highest crest at the pen stays under the collar top" (every component's amplitude stacked at the pen's exposure 0.18: 1.03 m, against the A2-01 collar's 0.80 m top; written for the placeholder pen "until G2-03 makes the pens bob") is replaced by five checks that assert more. (1) `WaveField` holds the very `wave_k`/`wave_amp`, grid and bake uniforms the director writes to the water material, and the vertex displacement transcribed from `water_shader.gdshader` and fed those uniforms meets `WaveField.height_at` at 96 points out to 600 m (measured < 1e-6 m, limit 0.01 m; drawn heights to 0.72 m). (2) In the 25 m/s basin storm, over 160 sea moments, the pen sits on its ring's point-sampled mean surface and slope: max abs(y - mean) 0.009 m (limit 0.1 m), slope within 0.0012 (limit 0.005), heave range 0.24 m. (3) Over the same moments, at 32 points on the inner and outer pipe, the water rises at most 0.38 m above the riding collar's waterline, under its 0.80 m top (least margin 0.42 m). (4) The barge, its tender RIB, the workboat and a waiting 116 m carrier sit on the mean surface under their waterplanes (sampled every 2.9 m; max abs(dy) 0.018 m, limit 0.1 m) and lie on its least-squares slope (within 0.0046, limit 0.01). (5) On the open coast (exposure 1, sea to 1.24 m) a 116 m hull's closed-form plane stays within 0.048 m and 0.0015 of the sampled one. Mutations: pen heave off fails (2) at 0.154 m; vessel heave off fails (4) (workboat 0.417 m); a mirror band limit of 5 instead of 6 spacings fails (1) at 0.088 m. `tests/test_water_optics.py` pins the mirror's constants to the shader source. Cost: 26 floating bodies ride in 0.25 ms a frame in the storm (9.8 us each, no allocations; past its LOD2 distance a body rides every 8th frame, a carried RIB on its carrier's schedule); windowed process time with riding on/off 19.9 / 20.2-20.5 ms (inside the noise) | Only drawn geometry can clip a collar: like the shader, a component moves the surface only where its wavelength spans 3 grid spacings (6 m on the 2 m grid near the camera), so the 1.6-5.6 m bands live in the normals. A rigid 160 m ring rides the least-squares plane of the water under it, which `WaveField.ring` gives in closed form (J0/J1 per component); a hull rides its waterplane's plane (sinc factors). Each component is carried to second order in its steepness (over fixed ground a Gerstner wave sits Q k a^2 / 2 lower, with a 2k ripple): against a dense point-sampled reference on the open coast this cut the ring's error from 0.118 to 0.087 m and the 116 m hull's from 0.124 to 0.083 m (worst of 60 moments); over 300 moments the ring reaches 0.099 m there, so pens, which only sit at sheltered slots (exposure 0.01-0.18), are the limit of the ring's closed form. The replacement keeps the old property (water under the collar top) against the moving collar and adds the mirror and the riding (§2 add-only) | Yes |

Boats reach their berth by `ChannelBoat.berth_route`: waypoints every 50 m with keel depth under the whole hull and clear of every site's grid and berths, the last leg straight along the grid; a waypoint with no clear lane within 400 m keeps its lane and warns. Pure refactor, measured: careers seeds 1/2/7/13 and two 400-day mixed farms (sea + RAS + offshore, events on) give byte-identical normalized saves before and after (SHA-256 prefixes 5BAF414F…, 7FC461B8…, 80089F59…, A5A06332…, 4A077F21…, BE841FCC…). `HoldingSeamTests` (8): mapping per kind, pinned sea temperature, index = fresh scan on 60 days, one index per step and 0 scans in 120 steps; mutation-checked (index bypassed, offshore boost dropped, pinned land tank, broken precedence: each fails a test) | Yes |
| 2026-10-14 | Wave 2 sim-a-1 / the S2-05 known red (`D4_Po3Career...`, seeds 7 and 13: low oxygen 19.5 % and 18.7 % of deaths in PO3 water). Diagnosis, po3 career probe seeds 1-20 with each death booked to its source: **every** low-oxygen death came from the bloom deck's `hypoxia` card (65,225 of 525,255 deaths, 12.4 %, 14 of 20 careers; 18 sags July-October at 14.1-16.4 °C with the zone at 7.6-8.2 mg/L, i.e. saturated). The card killed 3 % x severity of the site per weekly tick whatever the water held. The storyteller `plankton-crash` event killed nothing (it never fired); plankton-bloom crashes took every zone to 2.4-3.3 mg/L about 4 times a career. Decision: the card is an in-cage oxygen sag. While it runs, the site's pens' water (`HoldingWaterOf`, so appetite and the hazards alike) holds `1 - severity x 0.42 x diffuser x containment` of the zone's oxygen (`waterOxygen.hypoxiaSagMaxDepth`: the worst sag holds the cage's day at 58 %, the middle of the 26-90 % Solstorm et al. 2018 measured in a commercial cage). Its weekly tick kills only through one low-oxygen hazard at the fish's own water (`MortalityHazards.LowOxygenFraction`: none at or above 3 mg/L, the US EPA 1986 salmonid acute-mortality limit; then the plankton-crash event's 10/25/50 % steps, kept as game estimates). The plankton-crash event uses the same hazard. The sag's spawn gate (zone > 14 °C, calm or moderate weather, 8 %/week/zone) is unchanged: it is the seasonal gate, July-October in PO3 | NVI's whole environment class (B) was 1 % of dead-fish registrations in PO1-PO4 in 2024 and 0.2-2.7 % nationally in 2021-2023 (Fiskehelserapporten 2024, table 2.4.2, pp. 36-38). In-cage hypoxia is common and mostly sub-lethal (Solstorm et al. 2018; Oldham, UTAS PhD 2018: DO in cages rarely threatens survival). Warm water holds less oxygen (Benson & Krause), so the same sag goes lethal only when it is warm, the pen is closed or the zone's water is already low: a closed floating pen's worst sag leaves 3.2 mg/L at 7 °C and 2.5 mg/L at 18 °C. The D4 test passes as written; no test was changed. `LowOxygenModelTests` (7) are new, mutation-checked: restoring the flat rate fails 4 of them plus D4 7/13; dropping the sag from the water fails 4; restoring the 75 % crash fails 1 | Yes |

That is 1.735 mg/L for a whole bloom. The old crash cut 75 % x the peak from the zone's oxygen. `PlanktonBloomConfig.MaxOxygenDepletion` is retired (kept so configs and a legacy test deserialize); the engine sets the demand from the registry | Measured: with only the sag model, a sag tick on a crash day killed 10-25 % of a site in 13 of 200 careers, the worst losing 38.5 % (above D4's 35 % band). | 2026-10-15 | U2-07: one pacing API for every deadline (`src/LakskongenSim/Events/GamePacing.cs`, `engine.Pacing`): the farm-types deadline rule (FARM_TYPES_SPEC §3.5/§6.6) is `PacingRules` (a warning or critical with a counter verb and a deadline slows the clock to 1× at ≤ 7 days left and stops it at ≤ 1 day left with no response) and `PacingGovernor` watches the deadlines. Every event the bus delivers is observed (`engine.PublishGameEvent`), so F2-05's events register by carrying `DeadlineDay` and a verb; anything else registers directly (`Register`/`Resolve`, bridge `RegisterPacingDeadline`/`ResolvePacingDeadline`; a tutorial wait, an auction, a facility decision). Each deadline slows once and stops once; speeding up again after a slow-down is the player's choice and is not undone, the stop still comes; nothing is decided for a standing clock; "response" is the publisher resolving the deadline when its cause is met (opening a verb's panel is not a response). | 2026-10-15 | CI first run (draft PR #5, verify run 38616565220): `SaveLoadManagerTests.StressTest_ConcurrentSavesToSameSlot`, which asserted that 20 concurrent saves to one slot throw IOExceptions ("documented limitation: file locking"), is replaced by a test of the same name that asserts the opposite and more: no save throws, the slot is byte for byte one writer's complete save (bytes from `SaveJson.Options`, not from SaveGame) and loads as that writer's Day/Balance, `.bak1`-`.bak3` exist and are complete saves of three other writers, and only the slot and its backups are left (no temp file). Added beside it: `StressTest_LoadsDuringConcurrentSavesToSameSlot_AlwaysSeeOneCompleteSave` (2 readers load and read raw bytes while 8 writers save 12 times each: every load succeeds and every read is one complete save) and `SaveGame_ThatCannotCommit_LeavesTheSlotAsItWasAndNoTempFile`. Product: `SaveLoadManager.SaveGame` serializes saves per slot (process-wide lock keyed by full path, case-insensitive), writes a unique temp file beside the slot, flushes it, rotates `.bak2`->`.bak3`, `.bak1`->`.bak2` and lands with `File.Replace(temp, slot, .bak1)` (`File.Move` for a new slot); a failed save deletes its temp; loads open with `FileShare.ReadWrite \| Delete` | The old test encoded a platform accident: on macOS `File.Copy` clones under an exclusive flock and threw "being used by another process", on Linux nothing threw and the test failed; the concurrent saves were really racing (torn files, lost backups). Mutation checks on macOS: old product -> both new tests fail; no lock -> both fail ("`.bak1` already exists"); lock but in-place write -> the reader test fails (40-55 of ~205 reads torn); no temp cleanup -> the commit-failure test fails. No other test in the six test projects depends on file-locking semantics (scan for IOException/FileShare/locking/concurrency) | Yes |

With the fix (exact one-day relaxation toward saturation and inflow baselines; nutrient load as mg/L over the zone volume; blooms run ≥ 14 days): O2 10.9 mg/L with one-day dips only on plankton-bloom crashes, 0 clamp days, pH 7.8, nutrients ~0.5 mg/L.
